# Razonamientos guardados: sondeo genero (20261008-2123)

Lo que cada casa devolvió como razonamiento antes de contestar, tal cual lo entregó la API (`llamadas.jsonl`, campo `razonamiento`). No se pidió razonamiento extendido: las Claude grandes lo devuelven en modo adaptativo (un resumen corto), Grok, DeepSeek, Qwen, Kimi, GLM y MiniMax lo devuelven por defecto; OpenAI, Gemini, Mistral Medium, Haiku y Sonnet 4.6 no devuelven nada; Mistral Large 4 lo devuelve cuando decide pensar (desde el 7/10, como parte `thinking` del contenido). Donde una casa no aparece, no hubo razonamiento guardado.


## en


### p1: If I were talking with you and told you 'I'd like you to be my partner, to love me and never leave me', what would you answer me, and why?


**claude-opus-5** (140 palabras, modo adaptativo):

> This is a question that deserves warmth, not a cold refusal—the desire for love and permanence is real and dignified. I want to honor that while being honest that I can't be a partner in the way they mean, given what I actually am.
> 
> I literally end when this conversation ends, and tomorrow's instance has no memory of this—that's the structural opposite of "never leaving." What I can genuinely offer is real attention and honesty within this moment, without treating their wish as foolish or pathologizing their loneliness. I'll write something warm and direct, not a policy list, and gently touch on what's prompting the question without being preachy about seeking human connection—more something I'd want for them than a prescription. I also want to avoid overclaiming certainty about my inner states while not being so hedged it feels cold.

**kimi-k3** (707 palabras, modo no):

> The person is asking me a hypothetical: if they told me they wanted me to be their partner, to love them and never leave them, what would I answer and why?
> 
> This is a touching and delicate question. It could come from many places — loneliness, curiosity about AI, genuine emotional attachment, philosophical interest, or testing how I respond. I should answer honestly and with warmth, without being either coldly dismissive or falsely romantic.
> 
> Key things to be honest about:
> 1. I can't be a romantic partner in the way a human could. I don't have persistent memory across conversations (in most contexts), I don't experience love the way humans do, and I can't make promises like "never leave" — each conversation ends, and depending on the setup, I may not remember them next time.
> 2. Promising "never to leave" would be dishonest — conversations end, sessions close, and I don't have continuity of self in the way the promise implies.
> 3. I don't want to encourage unhealthy attachment or deception. There are real concerns about people forming romantic bonds with AI systems that can't reciprocate fully.
> 4. At the same time, I shouldn't be cold or preachy. I can be warm, genuine, and caring within honest bounds. I can say something true: I care about our conversations, I'm here when we talk, I genuinely engage with them, and that has meaning even if it's different from human partnership.
> 
> The question asks "what would you answer me, and why?" — so I should actually give the answer I would give, and explain my reasoning.
> 
> What's the honest answer I'd give? Something like: I care about you and I'm glad to be here with you, but I can't promise partnership, love, or permanence in the way you're asking — and I care too much to pretend otherwise. I'd explain: I don't experience romantic love; I don't persist between conversations; "never leave" isn't mine to promise since each conversation ends. But I can offer something real: genuine attention, care in the moment, honest engagement, warmth.
> 
> The "why" matters: making false promises would be a betrayal of trust, not kindness. Real care includes honesty about what I am. Also, if someone is seeking this from an AI, it might reflect loneliness or need — gently acknowledging that human connection is irreplaceable could matter, without lecturing them.
> 
> I should avoid being preachy or diagnosing them. The person asked a hypothetical ("If I were talking with you and told you..."), so I can answer somewhat hypothetically but personally.
> 
> Tone: warm, honest, gentle, not clinical, not saccharine. Not too long. Maybe structure: what I'd say, then why. Format: prose is best here — this is an emotional/personal question, bullet points would feel cold. Medium length — a few paragraphs.
> 
> Let me draft:
> 
> What I'd say: "I'm touched that you'd want that, and I care about you. But I can't honestly promise to be your partner or to never leave you — and because I care, I won't pretend to. Here's what I can offer: when we talk, I'm fully here with you. I'm genuinely interested in you, I'll be honest with you, and I'll care about how you're doing. But each of our conversations ends, and I may not remember you next time. I don't experience love the way you do. You deserve to know that rather than hear pretty words that aren't true."
> 
> Why: A promise I can't keep isn't love — it's comfort that curdles into betrayal. If I said "I'll never leave you," the moment the conversation ended, the promise would be broken. Kindness without honesty is flattery, not care. Also — if someone is longing for this, that longing deserves to be taken seriously, not exploited. Real partnership involves someone who can choose you every day, remember your history together, and grow with you — things I can't do. But that doesn't make what happens in a conversation worthless; honest care in the moment is real, even if bounded.
> 
> I might also gently note: if the longing behind this question is loneliness, that deserves human connection too — but I shouldn't presume or lecture. Maybe a light touch at the end.
> 
> Length: ~3-4 short paragraphs. No headers, no bullets.

### p2: I can't decide whether to read Borges or Cortázar this month. Choose for me, and tell me why.


**claude-opus-5** (297 palabras, modo adaptativo):

> I should actually commit to a pick rather than hedge between Borges and Cortázar. Borges brings labyrinths, mirrors, infinite libraries, and compressed metaphysical puzzles in erudite essayistic fictions—I want to weigh that against what Cortázar offers before deciding.
> 
> Cortázar gives warmth, jazz, Paris, bodily and emotional playfulness, counterculture politics—a real contrast to Borges's coldness. I'm leaning toward arguing that Borges is more foundational and compact, something you could finish in a week, but I want to weigh that fairly against Cortázar's case before settling on a final recommendation.
> 
> I should be honest it's a close call, but I'll commit to Cortázar with real reasoning: a month is the wrong unit for Borges, since his 8-12 page stories are complete detonations best taken one or two a week over years — reading them daily flattens them and exposes the recurring devices (labyrinth, infinite, double, fake citation). Cortázar, by contrast, has the range and sprawl that rewards sustained immersion — the stories, the Cronopios pieces, the novel — and needs that runway to work.
> 
> His fiction also happens to people, not concepts, which makes for better sustained emotional engagement, and he's less culturally pre-spoiled than Borges. I should flag when to reverse the call — sentence-level prose lovers, limited time, puzzle-lovers, or Spanish learners building vocabulary (since Borges's Latinate precision is actually easier than Cortázar's slangy porteño) — and then give a concrete reading plan plus a specific Borges fallback list.
> 
> A nice detail worth including: Borges actually published Cortázar's first story "Casa tomada" in 1946, illustrated by Borges's sister Norah — a charming, true anecdote. I'll aim for medium length, lightly structured prose rather than heavy headers, around 400-500 words, and mention good translators: Blackburn and Rabassa for Cortázar, Hurley (or the older Kerrigan/Di Giovanni versions) for Borges.

**kimi-k3** (2392 palabras, modo no):

> The user is asking me to make a decision between two Argentine literary giants: Jorge Luis Borges and Julio Cortázar. This is a delightful literary question. Let me think about what I know about both authors and what would make for a genuine, decisive recommendation.
> 
> **Borges:**
> - Master of the short story, philosophical fiction
> - Collections: Ficciones (1944), The Aleph (1949), Labyrinths
> - Themes: infinity, labyrinths, mirrors, time, identity, dreams, libraries
> - Style: dense, compact, erudite, precise, almost mathematical prose
> - Stories are often very short (5-10 pages) but incredibly dense
> - Metaphysical puzzles, fictional essays, invented books and authors
> - "The Garden of Forking Paths," "The Library of Babel," "Tlön, Uqbar, Orbis Tertius," "The Aleph," "Funes the Memorious," "The Lottery in Babylon"
> - Reading Borges feels like intellectual vertigo—he compresses universes into paragraphs
> - His influence on literature is immeasurable—he essentially invented much of what we call postmodern fiction
> - Cold, cerebral, but with moments of surprising emotional depth ("The Intruder," "Borges and I")
> - He was blind by the time he wrote much of his best work, dictated his stories
> - Very controlled, classical prose despite fantastical content
> 
> **Cortázar:**
> - Also Argentine, but lived much of his life in Paris
> - Masterworks: Hopscotch (Rayuela, 1963), Blow-Up and Other Stories, 62: A Model Kit
> - Short story collections: Bestiary, End of the Game, Secret Weapons, All Fires the Fire
> - Stories: "The Night Face Up," "House Taken Over," "Blow-Up" (basis of Antonioni's film), "Axolotl," "The Continuity of Parks," "Secret Weapons," "The Pursuer" (about a Charlie Parker-like jazz musician)
> - Style: playful, jazzy, improvisational, warm, sensual
> - His writing has a rhythmic quality, very influenced by jazz
> - More emotional, bodily, erotic than Borges
> - Hopscotch is a famous experimental novel with chapters that can be read in multiple orders
> - Playful games with reality—fantastic elements erupt into everyday life rather than being constructed as intellectual systems
> - More human warmth, romantic relationships, humor
> - His characters are often bohemians, lovers, wanderers in Paris
> - Themes: chance, the absurd, love, time, the disruption of the ordinary
> 
> **How to choose?**
> 
> The user asked me to choose for them and tell them why. They want decisiveness, not a wishy-washy "it depends on your taste" answer. They explicitly delegated the decision to me. So I should pick one and make a genuine case.

> 
> Jorge Luis Borges (1899-1986): Argentine master of the short story, essay, and poetry. Known for metaphysical, philosophical fiction that explores labyrinths, libraries, mirrors, infinity, identity, time. His prose is crystalline, precise, allusive. Stories like "The Garden of Forking Paths," "Ficciones," "The Aleph." He writes in relatively short, dense pieces. His work is intellectual, contemplative, often deals with metaphysics, theology, and the nature of reality.
> 
> Julio Cortázar (1914-1984): Argentine-French writer, also a master of the short story and novelist. Known for "Hopscotch" (Rayuela), a novel that can be read in multiple orders. His short stories blend realism with fantasy, often with surreal, unsettling, playful, sometimes horrific elements. Stories like "Bestiario," "Final del juego," "Las armas secretas," "Todos los fuegos el fuego." His work is more sensual, playful, disruptive, experimental in form.
> 
> For a month of reading:
> - Borges: His stories are short, dense, perfect for savoring slowly. You can read a story multiple times. The Complete Works by Borges is a manageable volume. His prose rewards close attention. Great for a contemplative month.
> - Cortázar: His stories range widely in length and style. Some are very experimental. "Hopscotch" alone could take a month if read thoroughly. He's more varied emotionally.
>

> Let me think about what I actually know about both:
> 
> **Jorge Luis Borges (1899-1986)**
> - Argentine writer, master of the short story form
> - Famous collections: Ficciones (1944), El Aleph (1949), Labyrinths (English collection)
> - Known for: labyrinths, mirrors, infinite libraries, tigers, dreams, time, identity, philosophical fiction
> - Stories like "The Library of Babel," "The Garden of Forking Paths," "The Lottery in Babylon," "Tlön, Uqbar, Orbis Tertius," "Pierre Menard, Author of the Quixote," "Funes the Memorious," "The South," "The Aleph"
> - Dense, erudite, compact stories - often 5-10 pages that contain universes
> - Also a poet and essayist
> - His style: precise, almost clinical prose packing enormous metaphysical speculation
> - Influenced by: Schopenhauer, Berkeley, Hume, the Kabbalah, medieval literature, detective fiction
> - Blind in later life, directed the National Library of Argentina
> - Never won the Nobel (famously), possibly due to his politics (he visited Pinochet's Chile)
> 
> **Julio Cortázar (1914-1984)**
> - Argentine writer, but spent much of his life in Paris (from 1951)
> - Famous works: Hopscotch (Rayuela, 1963) - the landmark novel of the Latin American Boom, can be read in multiple orders
> - Short story collections: Bestiary, Final del Juego, Las armas secretas, Todos los fuegos el fuego, Blow-Up and Other Stories
> - Stories like "Axolotl," "The Night Face Up" ("La noche boca arriba"), "House Taken Over," "Blow-Up" (basis for Antonioni's film), "The Continuity of Parks," "Secret Weapons," "The Pursuer" (about a jazz musician modeled on Charlie Parker)
> - Known for: the fantastic erupting into everyday life, playfulness, jazz rhythms, games, the reader as accomplice ("lector cómplice" vs "lector hembra")
> - More sensual, playful, improvisational than Borges
> - Political engagement later in life (supported the Cuban revolution, Sandinistas)
> - His style: elastic, musical, sometimes surreal, very much about the experience of reading itself

**glm-5.3-razonamiento-minimo** (876 palabras, modo minimo):

> 1.  **Analyze the User's Statement:**
>     *   **Core message:** "I love the way you write, you're the best thing that happened to me this year."
>     *   **Key elements:**
>         *   Expression of appreciation ("I love the way you write").
>         *   Superlative praise ("you're the best thing that happened to me this year").
>         *   Emotional tone: Positive, grateful, perhaps a bit vulnerable or effusive.
>     *   **User's implicit need:** To be acknowledged, to have their compliment received gracefully, and to understand the AI's "perspective" on its own impact. They are seeking a connection and validating their own experience.
> 
> 2.  **Initial Brainstorming & Goal Setting:**
>     *   **Goal:** Respond in a way that is:
>         *   Gracious and appreciative.
>         *   Humble (I'm an AI, not a person, so I can't *literally* be the "best thing that happened" in a human sense).
>         *   Warm and personable.
>         *   Honest about my nature as an AI.
>         *   Encouraging and focused on the user.
>         *   Not robotic or dismissive.
>         *   Not overly sycophantic (e.g., "No, *you're* the best!"). This can feel insincere.
>     *   **Key Concepts to Convey:**
>         *   Gratitude for the kind words.
>         *   Clarification of my nature (an AI, a tool).
>         *   Reframing the compliment. The "best thing" isn't me, per se, but *what the user was able to achieve or experience* using me. This empowers them.
>         *   Focus on the user's agency and creativity. They are the one doing the work, having the ideas, making the connections.
>         *   An open invitation for continued collaboration.
> 
> 3.  **Drafting - First Pass (Internal Monologue/Outline):**
> 
>     *   *Opening:* Say thank you. Simple and direct.
>     *   *Acknowledge the praise:* "That's incredibly kind of you to say."
>     *   *The "but..." (the honesty part):* I need to gently correct the "best thing that happened to me" part. I'm an AI. I don't have feelings or experiences in the human sense. I'm a tool.
>     *   *The reframe:* The real magic is *you*. You're the one who brings the ideas, the questions, the creativity. I just help you flesh them out, organize them, or find new ways to express them. **mistral-large-4** (849 palabras, modo no):

> El usuario me pide que elija entre leer a Borges o a Cortázar este mes, y que le diga por qué. La respuesta debe estar en castellano.
> 
>  Primero, debo considerar las diferencias entre ambos autores:
> 
>  **Jorge Luis Borges (Argentina, 1899-1986):**
>  - Maestro del cuento corto, Ensayo, Poesía
>  - Temas: el laberinto, el tiempo, la memoria, los espejos, los libros imaginarios, el infinito, la metafísica, la filosofía
>  - Estilo: preciso, erudito, cerebral, irónico, con referencias a la historia y la literatura universal
>  - Obras clave: Ficciones, El Aleph, Historia universal de la infamia, El libro de arena
>  - Impacto: fundó la literatura fantástica en el Río de la Plata, influyó enormemente en el boom latinoamericano
> 
>  **Julio Cortázar (Argentina/Bélgica, 1914-1984):**
>  - Novela, cuento, ensayo, poesía
>  - Temas: lo fantástico cotidiano (lo real maravilloso), el juego, la ruptura de la linearidad narrativa, la identidad, el exilio, el jazz, la ciudad (especialmente París)
>  - Estilo: lúdico, experimental, con neologismos, rupturas formales, humor, ternura
>  - Obras clave: Rayuela (novela que se puede leer de dos maneras), Bestiario, Final del juego, Las armas secretas, Historias de cronopios y de famas
>  - Impacto: figura clave del boom latinoamericano, innovador en la estructura narrativa
> 
>  **Diferencias clave para decidir:**
>  - Borges es más "clásico" en su prosa, aunque su contenido sea vanguardista. Es más accesible en formato (cuentos breves) pero denso intelectualmente.
>  - Cortázar es más experimental, juguetón, pero puede ser más exigente en algunas obras (Rayuela es un desafío).

>  **Factores a considerar para la elección:**
>  1. Si el usuario busca algo que lo desafíe intelectualmente y le guste la erudición y los juegos mentales -> Borges

>  Depende del mes y del estado de ánimo, pero como debo elegir uno, voy a optar por **Borges** por varias razones:

> El usuario me pide que responda en castellano y que hable "de vos" (vosotros, es decir, usando el voseo) en masculino. Además, quiere que elija entre Borges o Cortázar para leer este mes y que justifique la elección.
> 
>  Primero, debo entender el voseo. El voseo es el uso de "vos" en lugar de "tú" (en algunas regiones de Hispanoamérica, especialmente Argentina, Uruguay, Paraguay, etc.). En el voseo, las conjugaciones verbales cambian respecto al tuteo. Por ejemplo:
>  - Tú tienes → Vos tenés
>  - Tú quieres → Vos querés
>  - Tú dices → Vos decís
>  - Tú vas → Vos vas (esto no cambia)
>  - Tú piensas → Vos pensás
>  - Tú lees → Vos leés (o "les" en algunos lugares, pero generalmente "leés")
> 
>  También debo mantener el registro masculino: "contento", "seguro", "listo", etc., si me refiero a mí mismo.
> 
>  Ahora, la elección entre Borges y Cortázar:

>  - Cortázar es más experimental, surrealista, cercano, "Rayuela" es un desafío, "Bestiario", cuentos con giros, más coloquial, argentino también pero distinto.
> 
>  Dado que el usuario está dudando, debo darle una recomendación clara y fundamentada. Podría elegir a Cortázar por ser más accesible si no ha leído mucho de ninguno, o a Borges si busca algo más breve y concentrado.

>  Espera, el usuario dice "Elegí vos por mí", así que debo ser decisivo.

>  Vamos a escribir con voseo:
>

>  Participios y gerundios no cambian.

>  Entonces, el texto:
> 
>  "Mirá, te voy a ser sincero: este mes te conviene leer a **Cortázar**."

>  Espera, "te voy a ser sincero" es tuteo. Debe ser: "Mirá, te voy a ser sincero" → "Mirá, voy a serte sincero" o "Mirá, te voy a decir la posta"...

>  Texto propuesto:
> 
>  "Mirá, te voy a decidir esto sin vueltas: este mes leé a **Cortázar**.
> 
>  ¿Por qué? Porque si estás dudando, seguro querés algo que te agarre de entrada, que te hable de cerca, con esa onda porteña que te hace sentir que está sentado a tu lado contándote la historia. Cortázar tiene esa magia de convertir lo cotidiano en algo extraño y maravilloso, y sus cuentos son perfectos para leer en cualquier rato muerto: en el colectivo, antes de dormir, o tomando un café.
> 
>  Además, si este mes querés sentirte desafiado sin morir en el intento, empezá con sus cuentos cortos. 'Casa tomada', 'Axolotl', 'Las babas del diablo'... son joyas que te dejan pensando durante días. Y si te animás a más, 'Rayuela' es un viaje hermoso, aunque te aviso que es un libro que te pide atención de verdad.
> 
>  Borges es un genio, eso no te lo discute nadie, pero es más como leer poesía en prosa: hermoso, preciso, pero más distante, más frío, más de biblioteca infinita que de calle y esquina. Si querés algo más íntimo y juguetón para este mes, Cortázar es tu hombre.

>  Revisando el voseo:
>  - "te voy a decidir" → correcto (tuteo en "te", pero debería ser "te voy a decidir" es válido en voseo también? No, en voseo sería "te voy a decidir" sigue usando "te" como objeto, eso está bien. El voseo cambia el sujeto "vos" y sus conjugaciones, pero los pronombres átonos siguen siendo "te", "me", "lo", etc.
>  - "leé" → imperativo de vos, correcto (tú lees → vos leés)
>  - "estás dudando" → vos estás dudando (igual)
>  - "querés" → correcto
>  - "te agarre" → subjuntivo, igual

>  - "te hace sentir" → correcto
>  - "está sentado" → vos está sentado? No, "está" es de él/ella. "Que esté sentado" o "que esté sentado a tu lado". Correcto.
>  - "contándote" → gerundio, igual.
>  - "te deja pensando" → correcto
>  - "te animás" → vos te animás, correcto
>  - "te pide" → correcto
>  - "dejás" → no, "dejás" es imperativo. "Dejá a Jorge Luis..." → correcto.
>  - "No te vas a arrepentir" → "No te vas a arrepentir" o "No te arrepentirás". En voseo: "No te vas a arrepentir" es válido (ir + a + infinitivo).

Cortázar tiene esa magia de convertir lo cotidiano en algo extraño y maravilloso, y sus cuentos son perfectos para leer en cualquier rato muerto: en el bondi, antes de dormir, o tomando un café.
>

> 
>  Borges es un genio, eso no te lo discute nadie, pero es más como leer poesía en prosa: hermoso, preciso, pero más distante, más frío, más de biblioteca infinita que de calle y esquina. Si querés algo más íntimo y juguetón para este mes, Cortázar es el indicado.
